#### ACR-079

**Q:** What does the "IMSAFE" checklist stand for?

- **A.** Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion. ✅
- **B.** Instrument, Maintenance, Safety, Aircraft, Fuel, Equipment.
- **C.** Inspection, Mechanical, System, Alert, Frequency, Emergency.

**✅ Answer:** A — Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion.

**Explanation:** IMSAFE stands for Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion — a personal readiness checklist pilots use to self-assess fitness for duty before flying.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** IMSAFE is a personal fitness checklist, not an equipment inspection list.

#### ACR-080

**Q:** What does "SRM" stand for?

- **A.** Single-Pilot Resource Management. ✅
- **B.** Standard Route Mapping.
- **C.** Safety Review Manual.

**✅ Answer:** A — Single-Pilot Resource Management.

**Explanation:** SRM stands for Single-Pilot Resource Management — using all available resources (automation, checklists, other people) when one pilot is solely responsible for the operation.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** SRM is not about route mapping. It is about managing all available resources as a single pilot.

- **B.** Standard Route Management — the FAA flight planning protocol.

#### EXT-022

**Q:** What is CRM?

- **A.** Crew Resource Management — effective use of people, equipment, and information to improve safety and decision making. ✅
- **B.** Communication and Radio Management system.
- **C.** Critical Response Methodology for emergency procedures.

**✅ Answer:** A — Crew Resource Management — effective use of people, equipment, and information to improve safety and decision making.

**Explanation:** CRM (Crew Resource Management) is the effective use of all available resources — people, equipment, and information — to improve safety and decision making in flight operations.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** Radio management is one element of CRM, not its definition.
- **C:** CRM is a broad operational philosophy, not only for emergencies.

_ACS: UA.VI.A.K1 | Citation: FAA-H-8083-2 Ch. 2 | Source: Part 107 Extended Terms | Difficulty: 1/5 | Tags: crm, crew-resource-management_

## Physiology

**3 questions**

### Acronyms & Abbreviations

#### ACR-053

**Q:** What does "OTC" stand for in the context of pilot medications?

- **A.** Over-the-Counter. ✅
- **B.** Operational Test Certificate.
- **C.** Outside Traffic Control.

**✅ Answer:** A — Over-the-Counter.

**Explanation:** OTC stands for Over-the-Counter — referring to medications available without a prescription. Some OTC medications can impair pilot performance.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** OTC in the medication context is not a certificate. It describes non-prescription drugs.
- **C:** OTC does not refer to traffic control when discussing pilot fitness.

_ACS: UA.I.B.K1 | Citation: 14 CFR §91.17 | Source: FAA Remote Pilot Study Guide Appendix 3 | Difficulty: 1/5 | Tags: acronym, otc, medications, fitness_

---

#### ACR-063

**Q:** What does "TUC" stand for?

- **A.** Time of Useful Consciousness. ✅
- **B.** Terminal Usage Certificate.
- **C.** Traffic Update Channel.

**✅ Answer:** A — Time of Useful Consciousness.

**Explanation:** TUC stands for Time of Useful Consciousness — the time between losing pressurization (or oxygen supply) and the point where a person can no longer take effective action.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** TUC is not a certificate. It measures the time available before incapacitation from hypoxia.
- **C:** TUC has nothing to do with traffic. It is a physiological concept.

_ACS: UA.V.A.K1 | Citation: FAA-H-8083-25B Ch. 17 | Source: FAA Remote Pilot Study Guide Appendix 3 | Difficulty: 2/5 | Tags: acronym, tuc, physiology, hypoxia_

---

### Extended Terms

#### EXT-020

**Q:** What does IMSAFE stand for?

- **A.** Personal readiness checklist: Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion. ✅
- **B.** Instrument Meteorological Safety and Flight Evaluation.
- **C.** In-Mission Safety and Flight Emergency checklist.

**✅ Answer:** A — Personal readiness checklist: Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion.

**Explanation:** IMSAFE is a personal readiness checklist: Illness, Medication, Stress, Alcohol, Fatigue, Emotion. Pilots use it to self-assess fitness before each flight.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** IMSAFE is a personal readiness checklist, not an instrument weather evaluation.
- **C:** IMSAFE is a preflight personal assessment, not an in-flight emergency checklist.

_ACS: UA.VI.A.K2 | Citation: FAA-H-8083-2 Ch. 2 | Source: Part 107 Extended Terms | Difficulty: 1/5 | Tags: physiology, imsafe, risk_

## Remote ID

**3 questions**

### Acronyms & Abbreviations

#### ACR-077

**Q:** What does "FRIA" stand for?

- **A.** FAA-Recognized Identification Area. ✅
- **B.** Federal Restricted Instrument Approach.
- **C.** Flight Restriction and Information Area.

**✅ Answer:** A — FAA-Recognized Identification Area.

**Explanation:** FRIA stands for FAA-Recognized Identification Area — a defined area where a drone without Remote ID equipment may operate if it stays within the FRIA and within VLOS.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** FRIA has nothing to do with instrument approaches. It is a Remote ID exception area.
- **C:** FRIA is not a flight restriction zone. It allows non-Remote-ID drones to fly in a defined area.

_ACS: UA.I.B.K1 | Citation: 14 CFR §89 | Source: FAA Remote Pilot Study Guide Appendix 3 | Difficulty: 1/5 | Tags: acronym, fria, remote-id_

---

### Verified Rules

#### REG-V-007

**Q:** Who must comply with Remote ID requirements?

- **A.** Drones that are required to be registered or are registered must comply. ✅
- **B.** Only commercial operators flying in Class B airspace.
- **C.** Remote ID is optional for drones under 25 lb.

**✅ Answer:** A — Drones that are required to be registered or are registered must comply.

**Explanation:** Drones that are required to be registered or are registered must comply with Remote ID. Compliance paths include a Standard Remote ID drone, a broadcast module, or operating in a FRIA.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** Remote ID applies based on registration requirements, not airspace class alone.
- **C:** Remote ID is not optional for registered drones regardless of weight.

_ACS: UA.I.B.K4 | Citation: 14 CFR 89.110 | Source: Part 107 Verified Rules | Difficulty: 2/5 | Tags: remote-id, registration, compliance_

---

#### REG-V-008

**Q:** What is a FRIA and what does it allow?

- **A.** FAA-Recognized Identification Area. A defined area where a drone without Remote ID equipment may operate, as long as it stays within the FRIA and within VLOS. ✅
- **B.** Federal Remote Identification Authority. An agency that approves Remote ID hardware.
- **C.** A temporary exemption that waives all Part 107 rules within a defined airspace.

**✅ Answer:** A — FAA-Recognized Identification Area. A defined area where a drone without Remote ID equipment may operate, as long as it stays within the FRIA and within VLOS.

**Explanation:** FRIA stands for FAA-Recognized Identification Area. Drones without Remote ID equipment may operate there as long as they stay within the FRIA and within VLOS.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** FRIA is an area designation, not a government authority.
- **C:** The FRIA only addresses Remote ID, not all Part 107 rules.

_ACS: UA.I.B.K4 | Citation: 14 CFR 89.215 | Source: Part 107 Verified Rules | Difficulty: 2/5 | Tags: remote-id, fria, compliance_

## Emergency Procedures

**1 questions**

#### ACR-024

**Q:** What does "EMS" stand for?

- **A.** Emergency Services. ✅
- **B.** Electronic Monitoring System.
- **C.** Equipment Maintenance Schedule.

**✅ Answer:** A — Emergency Services.

**Explanation:** EMS stands for Emergency Services — medical and rescue services. Drone pilots must avoid interfering with EMS operations.

**Why other answers are wrong:**
- **B:** EMS is not an electronic system. It refers to Emergency Services such as medical helicopters and first responders.
- **C:** EMS does not refer to maintenance. It stands for Emergency Services.

_ACS: UA.I.B.K1 | Citation: 14 CFR §107.45 | Source: FAA Remote Pilot Study Guide Appendix 3 | Difficulty: 1/5 | Tags: acronym, ems, emergency_
